BC250 hardware video decoding progress
=====================================

Objective: get hardware video decoding working, and keep this text log updated.
User constraint (2026-09-25): minimize EEPROM/SPI flash writes and do as much
testing locally as possible. User clarified that BIOS flashing is authorized
without asking again, but only for a high-confidence fix. This supersedes the
temporary blanket pause; it does not justify a speculative write. Do not reflash
unchanged candidates or reset firmware just for convenience.
Times below are UTC. Detailed evidence is under output/video-decode-20260922/.

Current status (2026-09-26 21:03 UTC)
----------------------------------
User confirmed BC250 off and all five Pico GPIO wires removed; Pi showed one
RP2350 BOOTSEL device (2e8a:000f, bus003 device013), with the previous v0.2
program. Pi candidate SHA256 hashes verified. Loaded v0.3 UF2 using picotool
load -v -x and verified all flash bytes; only Pico flash was written. Pico
rebooted to runtime 2e8a:0009 bus003 device014. BC250 SSH remained offline.

Physical full USB self-test passed: 393248-byte packet, all393216 payload
bytes repeating0..255, flag80000000, CRC32 8b8adcb3. The v0.3 address-hunt
command also ran for1s with USB only and returned seen0/kept0/overflow0/
stall0/cancelled0. Copies: output/pico2/usb-selftest-v03.bcraw and
hunt-usb-only.json. Local candidate manifest updated; UF2 hash unchanged.
Next: reconnect the same five passive GPIO/GND leads while BC250 remains off,
Pico USB off; replug Pico USB. Arm key-range hunt, then APC-cycle outlet8 to
start a normal cold board boot. No soldering or BC250 EEPROM write.

active CS switch is not qualified. Existing passive v0.2 remained on Pico at

Six later 5.24ms windows at skips 20000, 50000, 242900, 256000, 270000 and
271400 were captured read-only. All complete READ03 bytes match working ROM,
flags0. No type50/type51 key reads captured. The address stream moves from

linear projection toward key9dad00 was wrong. Raw+decoded files are saved on
Pi and workstation under output/pico2/boot-warm-skip*.

Added experimental fast_reply.pio. It emits MISO's first bit during the
command phase of a preselected transaction. Assembled PIO, with synthetic
complemented reply, passes all10 digital timing cases on each of two real
recorded boot windows at modeled480MHz. This assumes original flash already
deselected, FIFO filled; CS selector/address check/fault logic and physical
overclock bench are absent. Do NOT connect as active to BC250.

addresses, core1 SRAM worker stores <=4096 hits and original transaction
indices; host validates range/order/overflow/stall. Two recorded-edge models

0cc5d0d7c0d36c74582d279959f78185e0d5bb9a0db0cfae9f60c8e212ddc280.

Previous preparation (2026-09-26 19:58 UTC)
----------------------------------
User connected Pico to J4004. Verified its passive v0.1 USB status, armed the
first full-size input-only capture (skip0, 98304 words, 120 s trigger timeout),
and instructed a normal BC250 boot. Capture reached DATA, but the host timed
out receiving the binary packet after 30 s. No complete .bcraw was saved.
The collection process exited1; later status confirmed the Pico still responds
and reports outputs=OFF. No capture or transfer is currently running.

Found a firmware transport defect: newlib fwrite buffers its final binary bytes,
while SDK printf/stdio_flush bypass that FILE buffer. Mixing the two can emit
DONE before the unsent binary tail. Changed header/data output to direct SDK
stdio_put_string with newline translation disabled; all frame bytes now use
one path. The resulting passive v0.2 ELF contains no fwrite symbol. Added an
explicit full-size USB-only pattern/CRC self-test, marked synthetic with flag
0x80000000. Host timeouts now save received bytes as .partial evidence.

All 34 local tests pass, including full-size USB framing and a regression for
a missing 32-byte buffered tail. Built v0.2 UF2 SHA256:
507da394603c7863971c9686cf89d79fd649a59c1b12118dc0e2416b45edd2dd.
Staged the fix and updated quick-start in the Pi kit. Physical confirmation
is pending. The user was asked to power off BC250, unplug Pico USB, remove
all five GPIO leads, then reconnect USB only in BOOTSEL for the update.
Do not flash/reset the wired Pico until this physical preparation is confirmed.
No BC250 EEPROM write, automated board power action or active SPI drive occurred.

Previous preparation (2026-09-26 02:12 UTC)
----------------------------------
Previous goal turn classification: progress (two-image build/kit, compressed
SRAM profile, 24 passing local tests and verified Pi staging). This continuation
also made progress: capture timing and repeatability checks are now implemented.

The raw decoder now records CS setup/hold, CS-high gaps, idle clock polarity,
and minimum clock/half-clock sample intervals for each transaction. Coincident
CS/clock changes are flagged, including deassertion at the final clock edge.
New qualify_capture.py re-decodes at least two raw captures against the working
16 MiB ROM, compares their common complete-read prefix and rejects CRC/stall,
ordering/byte/length, mode and timing failures. Every interval's lower bound
subtracts one sample for quantization uncertainty. Missing initial CS-high
time and unexamined tails are explicit. It does not infer type50 copy/check
identity, join separate boot windows, or output an armable board profile.

All 32 local tests pass; 14 hardware-independent host tests and all 20 listed
file hashes pass on the Pi. The two UF2s are unchanged. The 21-file ready kit
at /home/pi/bc250-pico2-20260926 includes the new checker and updated runbook.
The earlier overlay overview was corrected to describe the retained type50 /
signed TOS+driver route, instead of the superseded direct type51 replacement.
The signing key remains local and is absent from the kit. No Pico serial
device is present on the Pi as of this check.

Physical BC250 capture, full read-sequence coverage, copy/check pass identity,

Previous preparation (2026-09-26 02:05 UTC)
----------------------------------
The normal Pico workflow now has TWO images: bc250_passive.uf2 for input-only
address/data/timing capture, and bc250_interceptor.uf2 for address-checked
replacement replies. The interceptor also runs the isolated Pi bench. The old
reply-only experiment remains in source behind BC250_BUILD_LEGACY_BENCH=ON;
it is excluded from the normal build and ready kit.

Added a seeking phase: unrelated/short boot traffic passes with Pico MISO
forced off, until a complete first PASS anchor is seen. A second PASS guard
must match before any patch. Thereafter command, address and length are strict;
a malformed exact anchor or later mismatch stops replacement. Core1 now owns
cancellation too, avoiding a reset/enable race with the USB command handler.

Replaced the literal script with sequential PASS/PATCH runs and an SRAM-only
core1 FIFO feeder. The 638-row signed-payload replay covers all 318 changed
words in 15 runs plus 572 replacement words: 2468 bytes rather than 7656.
A full-object stress profile (both copies of type50, TOS and driver) expands
to 97354 reads / 1168248 literal bytes, but needs only 2636 compressed bytes.
The real board order is still unknown; these profiles are synthetic.

All 24 local tests pass. They use the assembled PIO, independent flash model,
the same host-compiled boundary policy and C run decoder as the firmware, and
the retained signed pair. Tests cover short/wrong/preamble traffic, strict
anchor handling, output release, full profile expansion and SRAM capacity.
The digital envelope remains mode0 READ03, 64 clocks, 150 MHz PIO, SPI<=5 MHz,
100 ns setup/half-periods, >=1 us CS-high gaps. FIFO service is modeled at
12/64/128 cycles and monitor actions at injected 8/32/80-cycle latencies;
these are assumptions, not physical timing measurements. 25 MHz still fails.

Linked-ELF verification expands actual compiled runs/payload and compares every
command/branch/reply with the tested script. All 3 PIO programs match; both
tables and the complete 764-byte feed/fault loop reside in SRAM, without flash
calls. The built interceptor UF2 is RP2350 RISC-V v0.4. Evidence and logs:
output/pico2/router-v3-20260926/; docs/pico2-router-validation.md.

The retained RSA key and signed firmware pair are unchanged. The key stays
mode0600 in the local mode0700 private directory; it is excluded from the kit.
The 19-file two-image kit and updated wiring instructions are in

isolated-wiring flag; the private key is absent and no Pico USB device is
present. Superseded bench/candidate UF2s were removed from both ready kits.

Previous preparation (2026-09-26 01:35 UTC)
----------------------------------
Implemented and built an actual command/address-checking Pico router candidate
in addition to the passive capture and earlier reply-only bench. It drives the
isolated flash CS#, compares the full read request before substituting a word,
checks transfer length in a second PIO block, and leaves an independent CS mirror
running after the script. Wrong addresses latch a fault before replacement data.
The supplied candidate is ISOLATED TEST ONLY: its sequence is synthetic, not a
captured BC250 boot, and it has no board-arm command.

The retained RSA signer and signed pair remain unchanged. Generated a 636-row
synthetic replay covering all 318 changed 32-bit words, including the original
key on the verification pass. The 7632-byte reply table is staged into Pico SRAM.
All 17 local tests pass, including the assembled router/guard/counter PIO against
a separate flash model, every short length 1..63, an overlong transfer, wrong
addresses/opcodes, script-end pass-through, and all signed-payload changes.
The modeled envelope is 150 MHz PIO, mode0 READ03/64 clocks, SPI up to 5 MHz,
100 ns setup/half-periods, 1 us CS-high gaps, 2..3-cycle synchronization and
4/12/32-cycle DMA service. A 25 MHz negative case fails. These are digital model
bounds, not physical Pico or BC250 measurements; CPU fault latency is unmeasured.

Linked-ELF validation checks all three PIO programs and every actual script word
against the model. Both the script and complete fault monitor are in SRAM; the
monitor has no flash calls. An SDK call initially left in flash was replaced
with the equivalent direct GPIO register update. The UF2 is confirmed Pico2 /
RP2350 RISC-V. See docs/pico2-router-validation.md for evidence and limitations.

Updated 20-file ready kit is staged at /home/pi/bc250-pico2-20260926. All 19 listed

Previous research and last measured board state:
The published Positive Technologies BC250 SPI interposer result changes the
earlier blocked assessment: it demonstrates a physical route for substituting
an authenticated runtime key and booting with re-signed PSP modules. Our offline
native tests now reproduce the required copy-then-verify split using this P3
image, and verify the exact TOS/driver signer relationship. The goal remains
uncompleted: our board has not run an interposer, accepted a usage-6 VCN key or
decoded a hardware frame. No high-confidence BIOS flash candidate exists.
See the 21:20 entry below and BC250_INTERPOSER_ROUTE.md for evidence/limits.

Normal Fedora operation is restored. The root-preserving video-key BIOS boots
after both a normal reboot and the user's PDU outlet-8 cycle. Both live key
tests returned FFFF0008 (missing key), contradicting the offline model's
FFFF300F prediction. Each stopped before the second request, restored the five
temporary controls, and confirmed that the SMU remained responsive. No frame
has been decoded in hardware.

Normal boot ID: 2b9446ec-9f94-4a73-a988-4f00f4bebc5a. Health at 17:14 UTC:
no failed services; graphics, CU restore, governor and tuned active; 16 threads,
schedutil and CPU downclocking to 800 MHz; read-only composefs and /boot;
SELinux enforcing. One-time diagnostic boot configuration and recovery timers
are removed/stopped. No flash operation or diagnostic job is running.

A read-only check at 17:15:46 UTC confirmed the 64 KiB modified flash region
still exactly matches the video candidate, including branch 05f08dbf. No
further BIOS writes have occurred. Offline analysis found an unchanged duplicate
of the original encrypted bootloader at 821000 in all seven checked images.
Our modifications were at the directory-listed 8e0400. Bootloader selection is
now the leading question: successful boot did not prove our modified copy ran.
A community SPI capture and a primary BC250 research report show reads from
821000 on other boards; neither establishes execution on this board. Original
handoff code already issues a full set/way cache clean and invalidation,
weakening the missing-flush hypothesis. No high-confidence flash fix exists yet.

A publicly shared PS5 boot-ROM dump now permits bounded native tests of the
selection step skipped by our earlier model. All 58 cases pass. Under historical
status 00080f1a, this ROM selects secure-container loader ffffb430, separately
from the directory loader that can load our patched copy. Four modeled early
crypto failures reach the fatal handler without directory fallback. This
strengthens the unused-copy hypothesis but is not a physical trace: the public
ROM's identity, the host/native register alias and early-boot state are not
verified on our board. Crypto calls remain explicit fixtures. No additional
hardware access, reboot, flash write or BIOS candidate was made for this audit.
Next: inspect secure-container validation and other entry paths locally before
considering any firmware change. Details: BOOTROM_SELECTION.md.

Follow-up: 28 native IRQ/FIQ and wait/transfer cases pass. The tested interrupt
handler returns to interrupted code; direct transfer requires mode bit20 plus
an explicitly supplied bit0 transition. No bypass or physical flag-change
mechanism was demonstrated. Official AMD4700S C0A has no video key in either
database, and both reject verification under our collected BC250 root. Its
directory type1 contains only "dummy binary", strengthening the separate-source
interpretation. Details: RELATED_SIGNED_FIRMWARE.md. No board access or writes
were made for these checks. Investigate existing authenticated loader paths;
neither an unchanged patch nor a foreign BIOS is a deployment candidate.

Runtime copy follow-up: 34 offline cases trace native source copies, hashing
order and signature checks. Changing a signed body byte before its private
copy causes signature rejection and a modeled destination wipe; changes after
the copy do not affect accepted body bytes. The VCN metadata tail is outside
the signed range, but this path needs the missing video key first. Scanning
675 installed firmware paths found 283 objects verified under existing board
keys (195 unique payloads); none has a nonzero metadata tail. No usable runtime
workaround was established. Details: RUNTIME_COPY_INTEGRITY.md.

Read-only board check at 18:31:07 UTC: same boot ID, system running, schedutil,
configured minimum 800 MHz, sampled frequency 2503674 kHz. No flash operation,
reboot, diagnostic module or firmware request was performed in this follow-up.
Hardware decoding remains unachieved; no high-confidence flash fix exists.

Another 24 offline cases pass for native destination descriptors and the

2026-09-23 00:01 UTC: driver memory initialization traced
-------------------------------------------------------------
The TOS pool setup initializes bookkeeping without clearing its DRAM pool.
Driver allocation clears page tables, then copies only 0x1a000 file bytes.
The later writable-memory clear loop requires header word >= 0x200; our
signed driver uses 0x100 and skips it. The cached video length lies beyond
the copied data. Six instruction-level cases confirm synthetic nonzero
contents can survive these paths; newer-header controls clear them.
This supports an uninitialized/stale-state hypothesis, not proof of the
live value or all earlier/later memory initialization. No BIOS patch made.
Caught a Unicorn memory-write-hook artifact in a Thumb zeroing loop; used
hook-free memory comparisons and recorded a separate reproducer. The
apparent extra writes are emulator behavior, not a proven firmware bug.
Board remains on normal boot d0f1bfd2-a2b4-437f-b0a6-16631830d7ba; SSH
health check succeeded, all four normal services active, no failed units.
Next: inspect driver initialization/indirect writers and find a bounded
way to establish valid video state. Hardware decoding still unavailable.
Evidence: DRIVER_MEMORY_INIT.md; psp-analysis/driver-memory-init.json;
results/driver-memory-init-health.log.

2026-09-23: teardown audit before preparing the live region probe
---------------------------------------------------------------
Seven new offline cases execute the actual DESTROY_TMR dispatcher/teardown,
slot removal and zero-descriptor routines. SMU responses, mapping and DMA are
models. Normal completion clears all three modeled regions. Injecting a mapping
or scrub failure leaves one or both subregions active while the handler returns
ZERO: its later slot-0 result overwrites the earlier error. Drain failure also
clears the main software state despite keeping regions active. A cleanup return
alone cannot validate live region removal. The prospective early probe must
attempt explicit teardown before buffer freeing, log it, halt graphics loading
and reboot regardless of apparent success; normal-boot checks remain mandatory.
Evidence: psp-analysis/trace-tmr-teardown.py; psp-analysis/tmr-teardown.json.

2026-09-23 02:22 UTC: native mailbox contract and RPC storage audit
-----------------------------------------------------------------
Previous turn classified as progress: actual client-transition execution
established sequencing, polls and ignored errors. This turn also yielded new
evidence that changes the planned live-test implementation.

Eleven actual SMU-instruction cases cover synchronous/asynchronous senders and
native try-lock. Sender lock calls, device completion, stack/interrupt boundaries
and barriers are models; this is not a physical concurrency test. Both senders
use native lock mask 20000, which Python transport locks do not acquire.
Asynchronous 1070 returns zero before completion. Synchronous 10d0 holds its
lock during an unbounded response poll and then truncates status to 16 bits.
Three actual TOS receiver cases composed with the sender traces show injected
SVC error ffff0000 becomes raw mailbox ffff0000 but sender return ZERO. This
does not establish that the live service produces that error; it establishes
the need to retain full status and verify device effects independently.

Try-lock 22ec immediately returns fe when held, including already-owned cases.
On acquisition it updates both global aee8 and current task+2c under the
interrupt mask, preserving unrelated bits and restoring PS. It cannot be
replaced by an uncoordinated host write of the global lock word.

Five sampler-prefix cases execute actual indexed loads with modeled LOOPNEZ.
Context 11e98 has pointer array 11f4c, mask array 1204c and shifts 1214c.
Count 14 reaches RPC argument start 12080, count 20 reaches result 12098, and
count 46 reaches code start 12000. Count 64 checks the larger loop. Actual
captured count is ZERO; nonzero counts/target values are synthetic, and accepted
live configuration counts or occurrence of a collision are not established.
This replaces the assumption that zero-filled RPC storage is unowned.
Native I-cache helper 30e8 is identified (IHI lines, ISYNC); installation and
bootstrap still require a concrete design. No code patch was installed.

Next: develop a bounded native sender that owns the transaction until full
response capture, using a controlled code/dispatch location and cache handling.
Do not use asynchronous return zero as completion, blindly reuse RPC storage,
write mailbox arguments from the host without firmware exclusion, or repeat
known hanging window reads. A completed transaction is still not hardware decode.

SPI; this is inferred from documented pin/protocol roles, not a physical board
test. Instructions remain conditional on confirming the actual chip and pin1.

Guide covers unpowered continuity checks, nominal3.3V check at chip8/4, avoiding
5V, BC250 PSU disconnected, stable chip ID C2 20 18/16MiB, protection inspection,
two full reads, cmp/SHA256 and comparison with saved failed-image contents.
Identical all-FF/all-00/repeating data cannot pass. Different full-image hash
requires byte-level review because settings could have changed during boot.
At512kHz, each16MiB read has theoretical minimum~4.4minutes before overhead.
Restoration uses pinned working controlf1251268..., full verification and a
separate identical full readback before removing clip and booting the BC250.

Additional source review of flashromv1.4.0 found prepare_flash_access invokes
the chip unlock callback for reads. The selected profile uses BP mask0x3C;
its callback returns before register writes if those bits are already clear.
The guide therefore gates reads on successful probe/protection review matching
the prior0x40/unprotected state, and stops on changes or unsupported queries.
No forced access or protection/OTP/QE change is instructed. No write command
was executed or hardware probe attempted. The embedded probe/read examples
passed Bash syntax checking only; the physical procedure remains untested.

Rendered and visually reviewed the SVG, corrected overlapping labels and
removed an ambiguous leader line. XML parses. Copied the guide/diagram into
the local Pi bundle and to the Pi's instructions/ directory, updated bundle
README/SHA256SUMS, and verified all five manifest entries remotely. Temporary
SSH control connection closed afterward. Root README/todo/OS image/outlet
script remain untouched. Hardware recovery and video decode remain pending.

Extended the prior direct debug-verifier test through native caller0x2f3c.
Twelve cases combine both images, memory fills00/A5, original/bad signatures
and skipped debug policy. Intact originals pass. Bad signatures clear the
object, leave debug disabled, set modeled status0x993 and return0. Skipped
policy returns0x76 without RSA. Fuse/policy data, platform ID, SPI copy and
hardware status side effects are explicit fixtures; SHA/RSA are software.
The later debug handshake and whole boot are not executed. This weakens a
simple debug-signature formatting hypothesis, not all debug-related causes.

writes modeled0xEE000001 to0x032000D8 and reaches spin instruction0x7CF6.

2026-09-25 15:12 UTC: bounded root-key/KDF dependency review completed
------------------------------------------------------------------
The previous work made concrete progress: external control restoration and a
verified healthy boot. Recovery is complete; hardware decoding is not.
New offline reproducer audit-root-kdf-dependency.py runs the exact control and
failed bootloader KDF wrappers through their continuations. 28 cases cover
both images, seven purposes, and two memory fills/synthetic AES-result patterns.
Native AES descriptor construction executes; enqueue supplies synthetic output;
HMAC is software using that synthetic key. Paired requests, PC traces and
fixture outputs match. Neither embedded nor copied root key is read in this
bounded path. This excludes a direct dependency here, not earlier boot-ROM
key provisioning, actual hardware AES differences or other firmware paths.

Two AES-error controls return native 0x1b from synthetic queue error 0x1a and
preserve caller output. Two positive root-read controls record all 32 bytes
from each root location. Initial harness error expected unchanged error code;
native 0x92e instead translates it. Initial read-hook positive control caught
cached emulator blocks translated before hook installation; final reproducer
invalidates them before tracing. All 32 cases/controls then passed.
Reproducer SHA25632c3e243280dcbc916306a1f57d3611dbcaf288b998986133672269b57d3188f.
Report SHA256fcf164136bf1c5b33661b923c0f62e789dd4300f8e46b302390b9873d9f82a8d.

2026-09-25 21:24 UTC: published BC250 interposer route reproduced offline
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
The user's Positive Technologies primary report is directly relevant, not
merely background. On another BC250, the authors observed 03 single-I/O,
4-byte SPI reads and repeated module-body reads, then used a Pico 2 with two
flash devices to give modified BL_PUBLIC_KEY bytes on the use read and original
bytes on the check read. They re-signed TOS and DRIVER_ENTRIES with their own
RSA key and reported Fedora boot. Primary source:
https://habr.com/ru/companies/pt/articles/979470/ . This supersedes the
overbroad conclusion that no authenticated path is available in principle;
their physical route has not yet been reproduced on our board.

The P3 type50 database begins at9dad00, marker at9dae08. Its usage30 signer
record at object+3f0 has key ID5a3587b59f4a4537a4c4c877db0ef505,
matching original TOS type2 at8eac00 and DRIVER_ENTRIES type28 at984f00.
The type51 database begins at9dbb00, marker at9dbc08. The installed encrypted
bootloader has not been decrypted; all native tests below use pinned internal
plaintext bootloader with explicit software DMA/hash/RSA and boot-state fixtures.

trace-kdb-split-read.py passed12 native controls across two memory fills:
changed body copied to RAM then original body supplied to the later hash passes
the original root RSA-PSS check, whereas unchanged modified flash is rejected.
Replacing the type51 usage44 row with the genuine Cezanne usage6 VCN2 key
produces native type13 VCN authentication success in the modeled downstream
path. This is a same-size substitution, not a boot-tested database: it removes
usage44 and leaves the original header's ordinary body digest stale. The
bounded installed firmware inventory found no usage44 object, but neither
boot-time use nor all firmware sources have been ruled out.

trace-kdb-signer-interposer.py passed4 native KDB split-read cases and8
independent module-signature checks. A disposable RSA2048 key replacing only
the type50 usage30 modulus is accepted in RAM with original verification
bytes. Original TOS and DRIVER_ENTRIES verify under the original signer and
reject the disposable key. Trial re-signed modules verify under the disposable
key and reject the original signer. This trial changes a non-executable TOS
body byte solely to exercise cryptography; no executable TOS payload, private
key or modified firmware file was saved. The generated key changes each run.

A new header control passed8 cases and exposed the exact restriction: native
hash initialization uses the copied RAM header, while body hash input comes
from a later flash read. Recomputing header+0xd0 body SHA256 to match changed
key bytes changes the signed header and makes RSA-PSS fail even when the later
body read is original. Leave the entire signed header original and change only
the first-read body. This accords with the primary report's invalid ordinary
hash on the patched key module before the interposer correction. Whether any
later component checks the stale digest on our board remains unproven.
The direct type51 record spans flash9dbda0..9dbeef. It differs in272 bytes,
starting9dbda8. Original/replacement record SHA256 are
2c0fa3fdb7b40386b0253740397fbd7328d77cef5069ee8b3080ae9c8abb2afc and
eba62408d67693e923a309ce62f108b563f78f02cb7f18b24a3ed060bc8ece58.
No full changed BIOS image was produced.

2026-09-25 21:57 UTC: preserving all keys with an offline TOS reset payload
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Previous goal turn: progress. It established the native copy/check split,
matched the P3 type50 signer to TOS and driver entries, and documented the
physical interposer route. The user now confirms they have only the Pi5 and
Waveshare CH347, with no Pico2, spare flash pair or SPI analyzer. A physical
interposer trial is not connected; the CH347 is SPI master/recovery hardware.

The native plaintext BL at405e loads type51 before its TOS handoff. The exact
TOS payload has a 1024-byte zero region at5c00..6000. A new476-byte ARM
component fits there and hooks original TOS instruction178 with branch
a01600ea. Native reset prefix98->140->178 disables MMU/cache before the hook;
the component reproduces the displaced mov r1,#24 and rejoins at17c. This
replaces the initial pre-reset-vector idea, which could have tried DRAM while
the bootloader's MMU mapping was still active; no such vector candidate was
flashed or retained as the final component.

The component guards original type51 $PS1/$KDB and three size fields at the
modeled DRAM address10784000, copies the authentic Cezanne usage6 VCN2
RSA2048 record after the existing usages10,44,17, and updates three length
fields. Sixteen native ARM reset cases pass: two RAM fills, two r0 inputs and
four header variants. Four positives make exactly84 four-byte key-copy stores
and three length stores, then execute the original reset path through stack
setup at190 with r0/lr preserved. Twelve bad-header/length controls do no
database writes. All four positive outputs preserve the three original keys;
the modeled native type13 VCN firmware authentication returns0. This is a
potential way around losing usage44 in the simpler direct substitution.

The same offline test runs two native type50 signer split-read cases. Original
TOS/driver signatures verify under the vendor usage30 key; a trial TOS with
the component and an unchanged-body driver-entries object are re-signed in
memory with a disposable RSA2048 key and verify under that key, rejecting the
vendor key. The TOS body digest is recalculated. The private key, modified TOS,
driver entries and full BIOS image are not saved. The signed-invalid type50
body still requires a physical check/use interposer, as in the published BC250
report. No on-board EEPROM write occurred.

2026-09-25 22:57 UTC: make the future SPI switch plan board-specific
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
Rechecked Positive Technologies' original BC250 article. Its Pico 2 PIO
switcher uses command trigger 0x039db8dc and a 0x338-command window after the
first body read. Those constants reflect its own BL_PUBLIC_KEY object; the
pinned P3 type50 body here ends at 0x9db8d0 (last 4-byte read 0x9db8cc),
while type51 ends at 0x9dc040 (last read 0x9dc03c). Do not copy the published
trigger/count to this board. Source:
https://habr.com/ru/companies/pt/articles/979470/.

Extended the Pi command analyzer to record every complete body pass's first
and last command index and the count between successive pass endpoints. These
are observed counts only, not safe switch settings; the input-only sniffer
cannot measure CS-high timing or MISO and may drop commands. Synthetic
complete/gapped/implausible controls pass locally and on the Pi. Updated
analyzer SHA256 4467cc76e0bc6e74135b98c974d16f3db6613664aa1c83f539168057fe1acfde;
test SHA256 0e3abe91ae1c19a93f34402d25977b6a0d983d7ca47bdf6bf111c84cfd768f84.

2026-09-26 00:24 UTC: evaluated Pico 2 plus original flash, without extra NORs
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
User proposed serving only changed bytes from Pico 2 memory while deferring
other reads to the original BC250 BIOS flash. This is feasible in principle,
but J4004 is a parallel bus tap and cannot isolate the flash. The original
flash CS# must be physically separated from motherboard CS# and driven by
the Pico; its MISO and Pico MISO may share the host net only when exactly one
device is active. A pull-up on the isolated flash CS# and Pico MISO high-Z at
reset are required fail-safe states. A one-original-flash socket/interposer
or carefully reversible CS# rework would avoid buying/programming two extra
NORs, but neither fixture nor Pico firmware exists yet.

Verified the pinned clean/patched direct-key pair again: 272 changed bytes
within the 336-byte record 0x9dbda0..0x9dbef0, first changed byte 0x9dbda8,
last 0x9dbeef. The complete type-51 object 0x9dbb00..0x9dc240 is 1856 bytes;
its body 0x9dbc00..0x9dc040 is 1088 bytes. Both fit easily in Pico 2's
520 KiB SRAM. The patch includes 530 bit changes 0->1 and 539 changes 1->0,
so a wired-low/open-drain overlay cannot produce it while the flash drives.
The flash datasheet gives 8 ns maximum CS#-high-to-SO-high-Z, and ordinary
0x03 reads have no dummy cycles. A Pico must preselect the source before the
target transaction and emulate whole 4-byte reads with PIO; deciding to take
over MISO only after decoding that transaction's 32-bit command/address is
not a validated design. The published BC250 two-flash PIO switcher does not
demonstrate Pico-generated SPI data. Our board's SPI clock and exact type-51
read chronology remain unmeasured. Documented the architecture, timing and
bench/capture gates at docs/pico2-original-flash-overlay.md and linked it
from docs/video-decode-next-step.md and tools/vcn-interposer-pair/README.md.

No board connection, SPI capture, flash write, reboot, PDU or Discord action
this turn. Hardware video decoding is still unverified and unavailable.

2026-09-27 13:13 UTC: signer boot and RAM-only VCN driver startup candidates
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
Recovered the complete earlier late VCN load kernel log: its type-13 request
returned 0x80000029 without any MMHUB page fault. The MMHUB fault observed on
early requests is not a necessary cause of that status. Re-ran the native AMD
video-initializer and destination-boundary suites (17 and 24 cases), which
continue to point to missing BC250 video state and TMR setup; they do not
prove silicon behavior.

Booted a signer-only Pico SRAM profile with type-50 modulus substitution and
fresh TOS/driver signatures, leaving both signed bodies byte-identical to the
working ROM. Fedora reached SSH on diagnostic boot
9b2bf3de-7107-4ab9-8257-b49ff8861de5. The Pico completed two full SPI
passes and verified 416/416 changed replies with zero mismatches, PIO faults,
stalls or late decisions. The one-time GRUB entry was consumed and removed;
no desktop restore was done.

Prepared two combined signer, VCN-key and PSP-driver startup profiles. Both
call the original PSP-driver initializer through a new Thumb wrapper. The
no-op control has no added memory effect; the metadata variant zeros the
56-byte video cache and sets the three Cezanne reference ports. Six native
startup cases pass, including clean/no-op equality beyond copied driver code.
The generated driver digest and both replacement RSA-PSS signatures verify;
profiles have 273/283 changed words and expect 293/313 replies per boot pass.
The ROM views are interposer inputs only, invalid as standalone BIOS images.

The first no-op Pico v01 UF2 verified into SRAM but failed to enumerate USB;
the BC250 remained off and was not subjected to that driver trial. Pi sudo
requires a password other than the supplied default, so remote USB power
cycling was not available. Asked the user to unplug/reconnect only the Pico
USB cable. Built and staged v02 UF2s with an early watchdog for future
pre-arm USB startup failures. Board BIOS and Pico flash received zero writes.
VCN decode remains unavailable.

2026-09-27 13:43 UTC: PSP accepts intact VCN firmware with video metadata
---------------------------------------------------------------------
Recovered the Pico after a non-enumerating v01 RAM image by briefly cycling
Pi 5 USB VBUS on GPIO42; the Pico returned to its installed passive flash
image without touching its flash or the BC250 BIOS. The v02 RAM loader now
loads and arms in one Pi invocation before its 15-second pre-arm watchdog.

Booted no-op and metadata-only PSP-driver wrapper profiles, each with the
signer and authentic usage-6 VCN key. The no-op control completed two SPI
passes, verified 586/586 changed replies with zero Pico faults, and returned
PSP 0x80000029 for an early signed-byte-tampered VCN request. Metadata-only
completed two passes, verified 626/626 replies with zero faults, and the
same tampered request returned 0xffff3072, the signature-rejection path, with
no MMHUB fault. Clock controls were restored after both completed requests.

A third cold diagnostic boot kept the metadata-only Pico profile. Its
intact navi10_vcn.bin type-13 PSP request returned ret=0, status=0x0 and
firmware address 0xf41f800000, the reported 4-MiB TMR base. The Pico
completed another two passes and verified all 1252/1252 accumulated changed
replies with no faults. The kernel hook deliberately stopped after this
request, leaving the GPU unbound, and the runner restored clock controls.
Raw boot/PSP/Pico evidence is in ignored output/pico2/type51-live-20260927/
driver-{noop,metadata,metadata-intact}/. No BIOS or Pico flash write occurred;
the desktop was not restored between trials.

Prepared a default-off BC250 VCN 2.0.3 module that uses the normal PSP
firmware path and an isolated diagnostic runner. The prior experimental
module used direct loading and would not exercise the PSP path that just
succeeded. A fresh one-time diagnostic boot is staged for the VCN driver
trial. No accessible VCN registers, running ring or hardware-decoded frame
has yet been demonstrated.
